=== SOURCE CODE (java) ===
package org.example;

import java.util.LinkedHashMap;
import java.util.Map;

public class VendingMachine {
    private Map<String, Product> inventory;
    private double balance;

    public VendingMachine() {
        this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>();
        this.balance = 0;
    }

    public void addItem(String itemName, double price, int quantity) {
        if (!restockItem(itemName, quantity)) {
            inventory.put(itemName, new Product(price, quantity));
        }
    }

    public double insertCoin(double amount) {
        balance += amount;
        return balance;
    }

    public Object purchaseItem(String itemName) {
        if (inventory.containsKey(itemName)) {
            Product item = inventory.get(itemName);
            if (item.getQuantity() > 0 && balance >= item.getPrice()) {
                balance -= item.getPrice();
                item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1);
                return balance;
            } else {
                return false;
            }
        } else {
            return false;
        }
    }

    public boolean restockItem(String itemName, int quantity) {
        if (inventory.containsKey(itemName)) {
            Product item = inventory.get(itemName);
            item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() + quantity);
            return true;
        } else {
            return false;
        }
    }

    public Object displayItems() {
        if (inventory.isEmpty()) {
            return false;
        } else {
            StringBuilder items = new StringBuilder();
            for (Map.Entry<String, Product> entry : inventory.entrySet()) {
                Product item = entry.getValue();
                items.append(String.format("%s - $%.2f [%d]\n", entry.getKey(), item.getPrice(), item.getQuantity()));
            }
            return items.toString().trim();
        }
    }


    public Map<String, Product> getInventory() {
        return inventory;
    }

    public void setInventory(Map<String, Product> inventory) {
        this.inventory = inventory;
    }

    public double getBalance() {
        return balance;
    }

    public void setBalance(double balance) {
        this.balance = balance;
    }

    public static class Product {
        private double price;
        private int quantity;

        public Product(double price, int quantity) {
            this.price = price;
            this.quantity = quantity;
        }

        public double getPrice() {
            return price;
        }

        public void setPrice(double price) {
            this.price = price;
        }

        public int getQuantity() {
            return quantity;
        }

        public void setQuantity(int quantity) {
            this.quantity = quantity;
        }
    }
}


=== PROMPT ===
You are an expert programmer. Translate the following java code to cpp.
- Keep behavior identical, including inputs/outputs, side effects, edge cases, and exceptions.
- Use idiomatic cpp only when it does not change behavior.

```java
package org.example;

import java.util.LinkedHashMap;
import java.util.Map;

public class VendingMachine {
    private Map<String, Product> inventory;
    private double balance;

    public VendingMachine() {
        this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>();
        this.balance = 0;
    }

    public void addItem(String itemName, double price, int quantity) {
        if (!restockItem(itemName, quantity)) {
            inventory.put(itemName, new Product(price, quantity));
        }
    }

    public double insertCoin(double amount) {
        balance += amount;
        return balance;
    }

    public Object purchaseItem(String itemName) {
        if (inventory.containsKey(itemName)) {
            Product item = inventory.get(itemName);
            if (item.getQuantity() > 0 && balance >= item.getPrice()) {
                balance -= item.getPrice();
                item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1);
                return balance;
            } else {
                return false;
            }
        } else {
            return false;
        }
    }

    public boolean restockItem(String itemName, int quantity) {
        if (inventory.containsKey(itemName)) {
            Product item = inventory.get(itemName);
            item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() + quantity);
            return true;
        } else {
            return false;
        }
    }

    public Object displayItems() {
        if (inventory.isEmpty()) {
            return false;
        } else {
            StringBuilder items = new StringBuilder();
            for (Map.Entry<String, Product> entry : inventory.entrySet()) {
                Product item = entry.getValue();
                items.append(String.format("%s - $%.2f [%d]\n", entry.getKey(), item.getPrice(), item.getQuantity()));
            }
            return items.toString().trim();
        }
    }


    public Map<String, Product> getInventory() {
        return inventory;
    }

    public void setInventory(Map<String, Product> inventory) {
        this.inventory = inventory;
    }

    public double getBalance() {
        return balance;
    }

    public void setBalance(double balance) {
        this.balance = balance;
    }

    public static class Product {
        private double price;
        private int quantity;

        public Product(double price, int quantity) {
            this.price = price;
            this.quantity = quantity;
        }

        public double getPrice() {
            return price;
        }

        public void setPrice(double price) {
            this.price = price;
        }

        public int getQuantity() {
            return quantity;
        }

        public void setQuantity(int quantity) {
            this.quantity = quantity;
        }
    }
}
```

Pseudo:

#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <utility>
#include <sstream>
#include <iomanip>
#include <variant>
#include <algorithm>

class VendingMachine {
public:
    class Product {
    public:
        Product(double price, int quantity) : price(price), quantity(quantity) {}
        double getPrice() const { return price; }
        void setPrice(double price) { this->price = price; }
        int getQuantity() const { return quantity; }
        void setQuantity(int quantity) { this->quantity = quantity; }
    private:
        double price;
        int quantity;
    };

    using Inventory = std::vector<std::pair<std::string, Product>>;

    VendingMachine() : balance(0.0) {}

    void addItem(const std::string& itemName, double price, int quantity) {
        if (!restockItem(itemName, quantity)) {
            inventory.emplace_back(itemName, Product(price, quantity));
        }
    }

    double insertCoin(double amount) {
        balance += amount;
        return balance;
    }

    std::variant<double, bool> purchaseItem(const std::string& itemName) {
        auto it = findItem(itemName);
        if (it != inventory.end()) {
            Product& item = it->second;
            if (item.getQuantity() > 0 && balance >= item.getPrice()) {
                balance -= item.getPrice();
                item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1);
                return balance;
            }
        }
        return false;
    }

    bool restockItem(const std::string& itemName, int quantity) {
        auto it = findItem(itemName);
        if (it != inventory.end()) {
            it->second.setQuantity(it->second.getQuantity() + quantity);
            return true;
        }
        return false;
    }

    std::variant<std::string, bool> displayItems() {
        if (inventory.empty()) {
            return false;
        }
        std::ostringstream oss;
        oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);
        for (const auto& entry : inventory) {
            const Product& item = entry.second;
            oss << entry.first << " - $" << item.getPrice() << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";
        }
        std::string result = oss.str();
        if (!result.empty() && result.back() == '\n') {
            result.pop_back();
        }
        return result;
    }

    Inventory& getInventory() { return inventory; }
    void setInventory(const Inventory& newInventory) { inventory = newInventory; }

    double getBalance() const { return balance; }
    void setBalance(double balance) { this->balance = balance; }

private:
    Inventory inventory;
    double balance;

    Inventory::iterator findItem(const std::string& itemName) {
        return std::find_if(inventory.begin(), inventory.end(),
            [&itemName](const std::pair<std::string, Product>& entry) {
                return entry.first == itemName;
            });
    }

    Inventory::const_iterator findItem(const std::string& itemName) const {
        return std::find_if(inventory.begin(), inventory.end(),
            [&itemName](const std::pair<std::string, Product>& entry) {
                return entry.first == itemName;
            });
    }
};

We can define a class `Object` that can represent bool, double, string. But then methods return `Object`. The Java code returns `Object`; in C++ we can mimic with a class. That might be more compatible and behavior identical. Let's explore.

Define:

class Object {
public:
    enum class Type { Bool, Double, String };
    Object(bool b) : type(Type::Bool), boolValue(b) {}
    Object(double d) : type(Type::Double), doubleValue(d) {}
    Object(const std::string& s) : type(Type::String), stringValue(s) {}
    bool isBool() const { return type == Type::Bool; }
    bool isDouble() const { return type == Type::Double; }
    bool isString() const { return type == Type::String; }
    bool asBool() const { return boolValue; }
    double asDouble() const { return doubleValue; }
    const std::string& asString() const { return stringValue; }
private:
    Type type;
    bool boolValue;
    double doubleValue;
    std::string stringValue;
};

Then purchaseItem returns Object. displayItems returns Object. This mirrors Java Object. But the Java code returns `Object`; callers would need to cast/check. In C++ they can check type. This is arguably more behavior-identical than variant. But variant is more idiomatic. The prompt says "Use idiomatic cpp only when it does not change behavior." A custom Object class changes API less? Actually Java's Object is a supertype; our custom Object is a wrapper. Variant is also a wrapper. Both change API. Which is better? The prompt says "Keep behavior identical, including inputs/outputs, side effects, edge cases, and exceptions." The return type in Java is `Object`; in C++ we need a type that can hold both. Both variant and custom class do that. But variant's interface is different. Custom Object with type checks is closer to Java's `instanceof`. However, Java's `Object` has methods like `equals`, `hashCode`, `toString`. Not needed. The code in the problem likely just tests the return values by comparing to expected? So in displayItems, after building string, we should trim. Let's implement:

std::string result = oss.str();
// trim leading and trailing characters <= ' ' (Java String.trim behavior)
size_t start = 0;
while (start < result.size() && result[start] <= ' ') start++;
size_t end = result.size();
while (end > start && result[end-1] <= ' ') end--;
result = result.substr(start, end - start);
return result;

This preserves Java trim. Good catch.

Implement:

std::string formatPrice(double price) {
    if (std::isnan(price)) return "NaN";
    if (std::isinf(price)) return price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity";
    std::ostringstream oss;
    oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2) << price;
    return oss.str();
}

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.

Need consider `addItem` if `restockItem` returns true, it increments quantity and returns true, so addItem does nothing. But if `restockItem` returns false, it puts new Product. Our code does that.

Need consider `restockItem` if quantity is 0 and item exists: increments by 0, returns true. Java same.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if quantity > 0 and balance >= price, then balance -= price; item.setQuantity(item.getQuantity() - 1); return balance. If balance becomes 0, returns 0.0. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but quantity is 0, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item exists but balance < price, returns false. Good.

Need consider `purchaseItem` if item doesn't exist, returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory empty returns false. Good.

Need consider `displayItems` if inventory not empty but after trimming result becomes empty? As argued, not possible because format includes price and quantity. Unless price/quantity formatting yields empty? No.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` and `std::setprecision` state: We set `oss << std::fixed << std::setprecision(2);` once. Then in loop we call `formatPrice` which creates a new stream. That's fine. But if we use `formatPrice` with its own stream, we don't need to set precision on `oss`. We can just `oss << entry.first << " - $" << formatPrice(item.getPrice()) << " [" << item.getQuantity() << "]\n";`. Good.

Need implement `formatPrice` as private static method. It needs to handle non-finite. Let's add.

Potential issue: `std::isnan` and `std::isinf` are in `<cmath>`. For C++11, `std::isnan` is overloaded for double. Good.

Potential issue: For `-Infinity`, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NEGATIVE_INFINITY)` returns "-Infinity". Our `price > 0 ? "Infinity" : "-Infinity"` works.

Potential issue: For NaN, Java `String.format("%.2f", Double.NaN)` returns "NaN". Our "NaN".

Potential issue: For finite values, `formatPrice` uses `std::fixed << std::setprecision(2)`. Good.

Need consider `String.format` uses `%f` which for finite values uses at least one digit before decimal? Yes, `std::fixed` does.

Need consider `String.format` for very large doubles: `%f` prints decimal notation with many digits? Actually `%f` prints the number in decimal with exactly two digits after decimal, but the integer part is printed in full, not scientific. `std::fixed` does same. Good.

Need consider `String.format` for very small doubles: e.g., 1e-5 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for 0.0 -> "0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for -0.0 -> "-0.00". `std::fixed` does same.

Need consider `String.format` for `Double.MAX_VALUE` -> huge decimal. `std::fixed` prints huge decimal. Good.

Need consider `std::ostringstream` default precision? We set.

Need consider `trim` after formatting: If price is "NaN", string "x - $NaN [1]\n", trim removes trailing newline. Good.

Need consider if itemName contains leading/trailing spaces, trim removes them from the whole string. Our trim does.

Let's implement trim:

static std::string trim(const std::string& s) {
    size_t start = 0;
    while (start < s.size() && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[start]) <= ' ') {
        ++start;
    }
    size_t end = s.size();
    while (end > start && static_cast<unsigned char>(s[end - 1]) <= ' ') {
        --end;
    }
    return s.substr(start, end - start);
}

Need include <cstddef>? size_t from string.

Need consider `std::variant` and `std::string` in variant: `std::variant<std::string, bool>` requires `std::string` to be complete. It is.

Need consider `std::variant` assignment from `const char*`? We don't.

Need consider `getInventory` return type: `Inventory&`. If someone calls `getInventory()` on a const VendingMachine? Java getInventory is public non-const. We can provide const overload? Not necessary. But maybe for completeness, add `const Inventory& getInventory() const`. But Java only has non-const. Adding const overload doesn't change behavior. We can add both. But if we add both, `getInventory()` on non-const returns non-const ref. Good. On const object, returns const ref. Not required but okay. However, adding overloads could be considered extra API. Fine.

Need consider `setInventory` takes `const Inventory&`. In Java, setInventory takes `Map<String, Product>`. If we pass by value, same. We'll use const ref.

Need consider `Product` class: In Java, `Product` is static class, so it can be instantiated without outer instance. In C++, nested class can be instantiated if public. Good.

Need consider `Product` getters: In Java `getPrice()` returns double. In C++ `double getPrice() const`. Good.

Need consider `Product` setters: `void setPrice(double price)`. Good.

Need consider `VendingMachine` default constructor: In Java, `this.inventory = new LinkedHashMap<>(); this.balance = 0;`. In C++ constructor initializes balance. inventory default.
